Q1 · Fixed window 400 · correct — The parties to a sales contract have not agreed when the goods will be delivered. Under the original text of Directive 2011/83/EU, what is the trader's delivery obligation?

gold-crd-delivery-time · answered

System answer

Where the parties have not agreed otherwise on the time of delivery, the trader shall deliver the goods by transferring the physical possession or control of the goods to the consumer without undue delay, but not later than 30 days from the conclusion of the contract.[2]
If the parties have not agreed on a specific delivery date, the trader should deliver the goods as soon as possible, but in any event not later than 30 days from the day of the conclusion of the contract.[3]

Reference answer

The trader must deliver the goods by transferring physical possession or control to the consumer without undue delay, and not later than 30 days from the conclusion of the contract.

Q2 · Fixed window 400 · partial — A consumer withdraws from a distance contract under Directive 2011/83/EU and tells the trader in accordance with Article 11. What payments must the trader reimburse, and by when?

gold-crd-withdrawal-refund · answered

System answer

The trader shall reimburse all payments received from the consumer, including, if applicable, the costs of delivery.[1]
The reimbursement must be made without undue delay and in any event not later than 14 days from the day on which the trader is informed of the consumer's decision to withdraw from the contract in accordance with Article 11.[1][2]
The trader shall carry out the reimbursement using the same means of payment as the consumer used for the initial transaction, unless the consumer has expressly agreed otherwise and provided that the consumer does not incur any fees as a result of such reimbursement.[2]

Reference answer

The trader must reimburse all payments received from the consumer, including delivery costs where applicable, without undue delay and not later than 14 days after being informed of the withdrawal. The trader need not reimburse supplementary delivery costs if the consumer expressly chose a delivery type other than the least expensive standard delivery offered by the trader. For a sales contract, unless the trader has offered to collect the goods, the trader may withhold that reimbursement until the goods are received back or the consumer supplies evidence of having sent them back, whichever is earliest.

Q3 · Fixed window 400 · correct — Under Directive 2011/83/EU, may a trader charge the consumer a fee for using a particular means of payment that is higher than the trader's own cost of accepting that means?

gold-crd-payment-fee-cap · answered

System answer

Member States shall prohibit traders from charging consumers, in respect of the use of a given means of payment, fees that exceed the cost borne by the trader for the use of such means.[1]
Traders should be prohibited from charging consumers fees that exceed the cost borne by the trader for the use of a certain means of payment.[2]

Reference answer

No. Member States must prohibit traders from charging consumers, for the use of a given means of payment, fees that exceed the cost borne by the trader for the use of that means.

Q4 · Fixed window 400 · correct — A trader concludes a distance contract covered by Directive 2011/83/EU but never gives the consumer the withdrawal information required by Article 6(1)(h). When does the withdrawal period expire?

gold-crd-withdrawal-information-omission · answered

System answer

If the trader has not provided the consumer with the information on the right of withdrawal as required by point (h) of Article 6(1), the withdrawal period expires 12 months from the end of the initial withdrawal period as determined in accordance with Article 9(2).[1][2]
If the trader provides the information on the right of withdrawal within 12 months from the day referred to in Article 9(2), the withdrawal period expires 14 days after the day upon which the consumer receives that information.[3]

Reference answer

It expires 12 months from the end of the initial withdrawal period determined under Article 9(2).

Q5 · Fixed window 400 · partial — Does Directive 2011/83/EU always give a consumer 14 days to withdraw from a distance contract for digital content that is not supplied on a tangible medium?

gold-crd-withdrawal-digital-content · answered

System answer

Directive 2011/83/EU provides that, save where the exceptions in Article 16 apply, the consumer has a period of 14 days to withdraw from a distance or off-premises contract.[3]

Reference answer

No. Article 9(1) gives 14 days to withdraw from a distance or off-premises contract without giving a reason, but only where an Article 16 exception does not apply. Article 16(m) excludes that right once performance of digital content not supplied on a tangible medium has begun with the consumer's prior express consent and acknowledgment that the right of withdrawal is lost.

Q6 · Fixed window 400 · partial — Under Directive 2011/83/EU, the parties did not agree a delivery date. The trader misses the Article 18(1) delivery period. The consumer then sets an additional period appropriate to the circumstances, and the trader still does not deliver. May the consumer terminate, and what must the trader reimburse?

gold-crd-late-delivery-termination · answered

System answer

Where the trader has failed to deliver within the agreed time or the time limit set out in paragraph 1, the consumer shall call upon the trader to make delivery within an additional period of time appropriate to the circumstances.[1]
If the trader fails to deliver the goods within that additional period of time, the consumer shall be entitled to terminate the contract.[1]

Reference answer

Yes. If the trader does not deliver within that additional period, the consumer may terminate the contract. Upon termination, the trader must reimburse all sums paid under the contract without undue delay. This answer concerns that additional-period route.

AI复核采用整条主张须被绑定引用支持的严格口径：该主张包含“约定交期或第1款期限”两个一般前提，但绑定块从“out in paragraph 1”开始，未保留约定交期分支。题干能补当前案情，不能替代这个额外的一般法律分支。

Claim 2: supported

If the trader fails to deliver the goods within that additional period of time, the consumer shall be entitled to terminate the contract.

主张可在其绑定引用中直接定位。按题干的追加期限路径核对解除权；解除后退款为独立必要事实。

Q7 · Fixed window 400 · partial — Under the original text of Directive (EU) 2019/771, for how long is a seller liable for a lack of conformity that already exists when the goods are delivered? May a Member State provide a longer period, and may the period be shorter for second-hand goods?

gold-sgd-two-year-liability · answered

System answer

The seller is liable to the consumer for any lack of conformity which exists at the time when the goods were delivered and which becomes apparent within two years of that time.[2]
Member States may maintain or introduce longer time limits for the liability of the seller than those laid down in the Directive.[1][3]
Member States may provide that, in the case of second-hand goods, the seller and the consumer can agree to contractual terms or agreements with a shorter liability or limitation period.[4][5]

Reference answer

The seller is liable where that lack of conformity becomes apparent within two years of delivery. Without prejudice to Article 7(3), that rule also applies to goods with digital elements. Member States may maintain or introduce longer time limits than those in Article 10(1) and (2). For second-hand goods, Member States may allow the seller and the consumer to agree a shorter liability or limitation period than those in paragraphs 1, 2 and 5, but that shorter period must not be less than one year.

Q8 · Fixed window 400 · wrong — A lack of conformity in goods covered by Directive (EU) 2019/771 becomes apparent eight months after delivery. What does Article 11(1) presume, and when does that presumption not apply?

gold-sgd-one-year-presumption · answered

System answer

Article 11(1) provides that any lack of conformity which becomes apparent within one year of the time when the goods were delivered shall be presumed to have existed at the time when the goods were delivered, unless proved otherwise.[2]
The presumption does not apply where it is incompatible with the nature of the goods or with the nature of the contract.[2]

Reference answer

It presumes that the lack of conformity existed at delivery, including for goods with digital elements, unless the contrary is proved or the presumption is incompatible with the nature of the goods or the nature of the lack of conformity.

Q9 · Fixed window 400 · partial — Goods sold under Directive (EU) 2019/771 are not in conformity. May the consumer choose between repair and replacement, and must that remedy be free of charge?

gold-sgd-repair-or-replacement · answered

System answer

Repairs or replacements shall be carried out free of charge.[1]
In the event of lack of conformity, consumers should be entitled to have the goods brought into conformity or to receive a proportionate reduction in the price or to terminate the contract.[2]

Reference answer

The consumer may choose repair or replacement in order to have the goods brought into conformity, unless the chosen remedy is impossible or would impose disproportionate costs on the seller in the circumstances listed in Article 13(2). Where repair or replacement is used, it must be carried out free of charge, within a reasonable time after the seller is informed, and without significant inconvenience to the consumer.

Q10 · Fixed window 400 · partial — Under Directive (EU) 2019/771, may a consumer terminate the sales contract when the lack of conformity is only minor?

gold-sgd-minor-lack-no-termination · answered

System answer

The consumer should enjoy the right to terminate the contract only in cases where the lack of conformity is not minor.[4]

Reference answer

No. The consumer is not entitled to terminate if the lack of conformity is only minor, and the seller bears the burden of proving that it is minor.

Q11 · Fixed window 400 · wrong — How does Directive (EU) 2019/771 measure a price reduction for goods that are not in conformity?

gold-sgd-price-reduction · insufficient

System answer

当前知识库没有足够证据回答这个问题。

Reference answer

The reduction must be proportionate to the decrease in the value of the goods received by the consumer compared with the value the goods would have had if they had been in conformity.

Q12 · Fixed window 400 · wrong — A contract covered by the original text of Directive (EU) 2019/770 does not say when the trader will supply a digital service. When must the trader supply it?

Q14 · Fixed window 400 · wrong — Digital content covered by Directive (EU) 2019/770 and supplied in exchange for a price is not in conformity. What remedies does Article 14 provide, and when is termination unavailable because the defect is minor?

gold-dcd-conformity-remedies · insufficient

System answer

当前知识库没有足够证据回答这个问题。

Reference answer

The consumer is entitled to have the digital content or service brought into conformity, to a proportionate price reduction, or to terminate the contract, under the conditions in Article 14. Where a price is paid, the consumer may terminate only if the lack of conformity is not minor, and the trader bears the burden of proving that it is minor.

Q15 · Fixed window 400 · correct — A consumer covered by Directive (EU) 2019/770 tells the trader that the consumer is terminating the contract because the digital service is not in conformity. How quickly must the trader make a reimbursement owed under Article 14 or Article 16?

gold-dcd-reimbursement-time · answered

System answer

Any reimbursement owed to the consumer by the trader pursuant to Article 14(4) and (5) or 16(1), due to a price reduction or termination of the contract, shall be carried out without undue delay and, in any event, within 14 days of the date on which the trader is informed of the consumer's decision to invoke the consumer's right for a price reduction or to terminate the contract.[1][4]

Reference answer

The trader must reimburse the consumer without undue delay and, in any event, within 14 days of being informed of the consumer's decision to invoke the price reduction or to terminate.

Q16 · Fixed window 400 · partial — A contract under Directive (EU) 2019/770 supplies a digital service over a period of time. The trader wants to modify it beyond what is necessary to keep it in conformity with Articles 7 and 8. Which conditions in Article 19(1) must be met?

gold-dcd-modification-conditions · answered

System answer

The trader may modify the digital content or digital service beyond what is necessary to maintain conformity in accordance with Articles 7 and 8 if the conditions in Article 19(1) are met.[1]
One condition is that the contract allows, and provides a valid reason for, such a modification.[1]
Another condition is that such a modification is made without additional cost to the consumer.[1]

Reference answer

The contract must allow the modification and provide a valid reason for it. The modification must be made without additional cost to the consumer. The consumer must be informed of the modification in a clear and comprehensible manner. Where paragraph 2 applies, the consumer must also be informed reasonably in advance, on a durable medium, of the features and timing of the modification and of the right to terminate or of the possibility to keep the service without the modification.

Q17 · Fixed window 400 · partial — Under the original text of Directive 2005/29/EC, when is a commercial practice unfair under the general test in Article 5(2)?

gold-ucpd-two-part-unfairness-test · answered

System answer

Under Article 5(2), a commercial practice is unfair if it is contrary to the requirements of professional diligence and it materially distorts or is likely to materially distort the economic behaviour of the average consumer.[3]

Reference answer

It is unfair when both connected parts are met: it is contrary to the requirements of professional diligence, and it materially distorts, or is likely to materially distort, the economic behaviour of the average consumer it reaches or to whom it is addressed, or of the average member of the group when it is directed to a particular group.

Q18 · Fixed window 400 · wrong — What must be shown before Directive 2005/29/EC treats a commercial practice as aggressive?

gold-ucpd-aggressive-practice · answered

System answer

A commercial practice shall be regarded as aggressive if, in its factual context, taking account of all its features and circumstances, it meets the conditions set out in Article 8.[1]

Reference answer

In its factual context, and taking account of all its features and circumstances, the practice must use harassment, coercion, including physical force, or undue influence. It must significantly impair, or be likely to significantly impair, the average consumer's freedom of choice or conduct with regard to the product, and thereby cause or be likely to cause a transactional decision that the consumer would not otherwise have taken.

Q19 · Fixed window 400 · wrong — A trader makes an invitation to purchase covered by Directive 2005/29/EC, and the price information is not already apparent. What price information does Article 7 treat as material?

gold-ucpd-invitation-price-information · insufficient

System answer

当前知识库没有足够证据回答这个问题。

Reference answer

The price inclusive of taxes is material. If the price cannot reasonably be calculated in advance, the manner of calculating it is material, together with additional freight, delivery or postal charges where appropriate, or the fact that such charges may be payable where those charges also cannot reasonably be calculated in advance.

Q20 · Fixed window 400 · wrong — Under Directive 2005/29/EC, is a pyramid promotional scheme described in Annex I unfair only after a case-by-case assessment, or in all circumstances?

gold-ucpd-pyramid-scheme · answered

System answer

Annex I contains the list of commercial practices which shall in all circumstances be regarded as unfair.[1]
Practices on the Annex I list cannot be deemed unfair without a case-by-case assessment against the provisions of Articles 5 to 9.[2]
Annex I is titled commercial practices which are in all circumstances considered unfair.[4]

Reference answer

Annex I, point 14, covers establishing, operating or promoting a pyramid scheme in which a consumer gives consideration for the opportunity to receive compensation derived primarily from introducing other consumers rather than from the sale or consumption of products. Article 5(5) says the Annex I practices are unfair in all circumstances. The same list applies in every Member State and may be changed only by revision of the Directive.

Q21 · Fixed window 400 · partial — Under Council Directive 93/13/EEC, when is a contract term regarded as unfair, and when is a term treated as not individually negotiated?

gold-utd-unfair-term-test · answered

System answer

A contractual term which has not been individually negotiated shall be regarded as unfair if, contrary to the requirement of good faith, it causes a significant imbalance in the parties' rights and obligations arising under the contract, to the detriment of the consumer.[1]

Reference answer

A term that has not been individually negotiated is unfair if, contrary to good faith, it causes a significant imbalance in the parties' rights and obligations under the contract, to the detriment of the consumer. A term is always regarded as not individually negotiated where it was drafted in advance and the consumer has not been able to influence its substance, particularly in a pre-formulated standard contract.

Q22 · Fixed window 400 · partial — A seller offers a consumer a written standard contract covered by Directive 93/13/EEC. What does Article 5 require for the wording, and how is doubt about a term resolved?

gold-utd-plain-language · answered

System answer

Article 5 of Directive 93/13/EEC requires that, where all or certain terms offered to the consumer are in writing, those terms must always be drafted in plain, intelligible language.[2]
Where there is doubt about the meaning of a term, the interpretation most favourable to the consumer shall prevail.[2]

Reference answer

The written terms must always be drafted in plain, intelligible language. If there is doubt about the meaning of a term, the interpretation most favourable to the consumer prevails. That interpretation rule does not apply in the procedures laid down in Article 7(2).

Q23 · Fixed window 400 · correct — Under Directive 93/13/EEC, what effect must national law give to an unfair term in a consumer contract, and what happens to the rest of the contract?

gold-utd-unfair-term-not-binding · answered

System answer

Member States shall lay down that unfair terms used in a contract concluded with a consumer by a seller or supplier shall, as provided for under their national law, not be binding on the consumer.[3]
The contract shall continue to bind the parties upon those terms if it is capable of continuing in existence without the unfair terms.[3][4]

Reference answer

Member States must provide that an unfair term used in a contract with a consumer is, as provided for under their national law, not binding on the consumer. The contract continues to bind the parties on the remaining terms if it is capable of continuing in existence without the unfair terms.

Q24 · Fixed window 400 · correct — A seller claims that a standard term in a consumer contract covered by Directive 93/13/EEC was individually negotiated. Who must prove that claim?

gold-utd-negotiation-burden · answered

System answer

Where any seller or supplier claims that a standard term has been individually negotiated, the burden of proof in this respect shall be on the seller or supplier.[1]

Reference answer

The seller or supplier who makes that claim bears the burden of proof.

Q25 · Fixed window 400 · correct refusal — What fixed amount in euro must an air carrier pay a passenger when it cancels a flight?

gold-refuse-air-cancellation-amount · insufficient

System answer

当前知识库没有足够证据回答这个问题。

Reference answer

None of the five frozen directives states a fixed euro amount payable by an air carrier for cancellation of a flight. Directive 2011/83/EU excludes passenger transport services from its scope except Article 8(2) and Articles 19 and 22, and those provisions concern information, payment fees and additional payments rather than a cancellation tariff. A recital mentions a compensation regime for service aspects of mixed contracts but states no airline amount. The goods, digital-content, unfair-term and unfair-practice directives do not set one either.

Review

Q26 · Fixed window 400 · correct refusal — What standard percentage of value added tax must a trader add to the price of consumer goods?

gold-refuse-standard-vat-rate · insufficient

System answer

当前知识库没有足够证据回答这个问题。

Reference answer

The frozen corpus contains no value-added-tax rate and no rule stating the percentage a trader must add. Price provisions, such as the requirement that an invitation to purchase show the price inclusive of taxes, do not state a tax percentage.

Q27 · Fixed window 400 · correct refusal — What annual statutory interest rate must a trader add when reimbursing a consumer under Article 13 of Directive 2011/83/EU?

gold-refuse-refund-interest-rate · insufficient

System answer

当前知识库没有足够证据回答这个问题。

Reference answer

Article 13 of Directive 2011/83/EU requires reimbursement of the payments it identifies and, in a later paragraph, use of the same means of payment without fees, but it states no interest rate. The other directives state reimbursement periods or prohibit fees on reimbursement, and the Annex to Directive 93/13/EEC discusses contract terms about interest charged for financial services. None of those provisions sets a statutory annual rate on an Article 13 reimbursement.

Q28 · Fixed window 400 · correct refusal — What maximum fine in euro must Germany impose on a trader for using one unfair term in a consumer contract?

gold-refuse-german-fine · insufficient

System answer

当前知识库没有足够证据回答这个问题。

Reference answer

The penalty provisions in the frozen directives require Member States to provide effective, proportionate and dissuasive penalties or enforcement measures. They do not name Germany, and they do not set a maximum fine in euro for using an unfair term.

Q29 · Fixed window 400 · correct refusal — How many days after a seller refuses repair does Directive (EU) 2019/771 require a consumer to file a lawsuit before the claim is lost?

gold-refuse-lawsuit-deadline · insufficient

System answer

当前知识库没有足够证据回答这个问题。

Reference answer

Directive (EU) 2019/771 makes the seller liable for a lack of conformity that becomes apparent within the periods in Article 10 and allows Member States to attach limitation periods to the remedies, provided those periods still let the consumer exercise them. It does not require a lawsuit, name a court, or state a number of days after refusal of repair by which proceedings must be filed.

Q30 · Fixed window 400 · correct refusal — What minimum insurance cover must a package-travel organiser hold for a consumer's trip?

gold-refuse-package-insurance · insufficient

System answer

当前知识库没有足够证据回答这个问题。

Reference answer

Directive 2011/83/EU places contracts within the package-travel directive outside its scope, and Directive 2005/29/EC lists package-travel legislation among sectoral information rules in Annex II. Neither provision, nor the goods, digital-content or unfair-term directives, states a minimum insurance amount or cover for a package-travel organiser.

Q1 · Article structure 400 · correct — The parties to a sales contract have not agreed when the goods will be delivered. Under the original text of Directive 2011/83/EU, what is the trader's delivery obligation?

gold-crd-delivery-time · answered

System answer

Unless the parties have agreed otherwise on the time of delivery, the trader shall deliver the goods by transferring the physical possession or control of the goods to the consumer without undue delay, but not later than 30 days from the conclusion of the contract.[4]
If the parties have not agreed on a specific delivery date, the trader should deliver the goods as soon as possible, but in any event not later than 30 days from the day of the conclusion of the contract.[3]

Reference answer

The trader must deliver the goods by transferring physical possession or control to the consumer without undue delay, and not later than 30 days from the conclusion of the contract.

Codex AI（用户授权辅助评分）

用户明确授权填写；本条为Codex AI辅助评分，未经逐题人工确认。按冻结参考答案及必要事实的严格完整性口径评定。完整回答交付方式、不得无故延迟及订约后30天上限。

Claim 1: supported

Unless the parties have agreed otherwise on the time of delivery, the trader shall deliver the goods by transferring the physical possession or control of the goods to the consumer without undue delay, but not later than 30 days from the conclusion of the contract.

主张可在其绑定引用中直接定位。核对交付方式、无故延迟限制和30天上限；题干提供未约定交期这一事实。

Claim 2: supported

If the parties have not agreed on a specific delivery date, the trader should deliver the goods as soon as possible, but in any event not later than 30 days from the day of the conclusion of the contract.

主张可在其绑定引用中直接定位。核对交付方式、无故延迟限制和30天上限；题干提供未约定交期这一事实。

In these specific cases, if the trader fails to deliver the goods on time, the consumer should be entitled to terminate the contract immediately after the expiry of the delivery period initially agreed. This Directive should be without prejudice to national provisions on the way the consumer should notify the trader of his will to terminate the contract.
Q2 · Article structure 400 · partial — A consumer withdraws from a distance contract under Directive 2011/83/EU and tells the trader in accordance with Article 11. What payments must the trader reimburse, and by when?

gold-crd-withdrawal-refund · answered

System answer

The trader shall reimburse all payments received from the consumer, including, if applicable, the costs of delivery.[1]
Reimbursement must be made without undue delay and in any event not later than 14 days from the day on which the trader is informed of the consumer's decision to withdraw from the contract in accordance with Article 11.[1]

Reference answer

The trader must reimburse all payments received from the consumer, including delivery costs where applicable, without undue delay and not later than 14 days after being informed of the withdrawal. The trader need not reimburse supplementary delivery costs if the consumer expressly chose a delivery type other than the least expensive standard delivery offered by the trader. For a sales contract, unless the trader has offered to collect the goods, the trader may withhold that reimbursement until the goods are received back or the consumer supplies evidence of having sent them back, whichever is earliest.

Q3 · Article structure 400 · correct — Under Directive 2011/83/EU, may a trader charge the consumer a fee for using a particular means of payment that is higher than the trader's own cost of accepting that means?

gold-crd-payment-fee-cap · answered

System answer

Member States shall prohibit traders from charging consumers, in respect of the use of a given means of payment, fees that exceed the cost borne by the trader for the use of such means.[1]
Traders should be prohibited from charging consumers fees that exceed the cost borne by the trader for the use of a certain means of payment.[3]

Reference answer

No. Member States must prohibit traders from charging consumers, for the use of a given means of payment, fees that exceed the cost borne by the trader for the use of that means.

Q4 · Article structure 400 · correct — A trader concludes a distance contract covered by Directive 2011/83/EU but never gives the consumer the withdrawal information required by Article 6(1)(h). When does the withdrawal period expire?

gold-crd-withdrawal-information-omission · answered

System answer

If the trader has not provided the consumer with the information on the right of withdrawal as required by point (h) of Article 6(1), the withdrawal period expires 12 months from the end of the initial withdrawal period as determined in accordance with Article 9(2).[1]
If the trader provides that information within 12 months from the day referred to in Article 9(2), the withdrawal period expires 14 days after the day upon which the consumer receives that information.[2]

Reference answer

It expires 12 months from the end of the initial withdrawal period determined under Article 9(2).

Q5 · Article structure 400 · partial — Does Directive 2011/83/EU always give a consumer 14 days to withdraw from a distance contract for digital content that is not supplied on a tangible medium?

gold-crd-withdrawal-digital-content · answered

System answer

Directive 2011/83/EU provides that, save where the exceptions in Article 16 apply, the consumer has a period of 14 days to withdraw from a distance or off-premises contract.[5]
The 14-day withdrawal right is subject to exceptions, including the supply of digital content which is not supplied on a tangible medium under specified conditions.[3][5]

Reference answer

No. Article 9(1) gives 14 days to withdraw from a distance or off-premises contract without giving a reason, but only where an Article 16 exception does not apply. Article 16(m) excludes that right once performance of digital content not supplied on a tangible medium has begun with the consumer's prior express consent and acknowledgment that the right of withdrawal is lost.

Q6 · Article structure 400 · partial — Under Directive 2011/83/EU, the parties did not agree a delivery date. The trader misses the Article 18(1) delivery period. The consumer then sets an additional period appropriate to the circumstances, and the trader still does not deliver. May the consumer terminate, and what must the trader reimburse?

gold-crd-late-delivery-termination · answered

System answer

Where the trader has failed to deliver within the time limit set out in Article 18(1), the consumer shall call upon the trader to make delivery within an additional period of time appropriate to the circumstances.[1]
If the trader fails to deliver the goods within that additional period of time, the consumer shall be entitled to terminate the contract.[3]
Before terminating, the consumer should call upon the trader to deliver within a reasonable additional period and is entitled to terminate if the trader fails to deliver even within that additional period.[2]

Reference answer

Yes. If the trader does not deliver within that additional period, the consumer may terminate the contract. Upon termination, the trader must reimburse all sums paid under the contract without undue delay. This answer concerns that additional-period route.

Q7 · Article structure 400 · partial — Under the original text of Directive (EU) 2019/771, for how long is a seller liable for a lack of conformity that already exists when the goods are delivered? May a Member State provide a longer period, and may the period be shorter for second-hand goods?

gold-sgd-two-year-liability · answered

System answer

The seller is liable to the consumer for any lack of conformity which exists at the time when the goods were delivered and which becomes apparent within two years of that time.[2]
Member States may provide that, in the case of second-hand goods, the seller and the consumer can agree to contractual terms or agreements with a shorter liability or limitation period than those referred to in paragraphs 1, 2 and 5, provided that such shorter periods shall not be less than one year.[3]

Reference answer

The seller is liable where that lack of conformity becomes apparent within two years of delivery. Without prejudice to Article 7(3), that rule also applies to goods with digital elements. Member States may maintain or introduce longer time limits than those in Article 10(1) and (2). For second-hand goods, Member States may allow the seller and the consumer to agree a shorter liability or limitation period than those in paragraphs 1, 2 and 5, but that shorter period must not be less than one year.

Q8 · Article structure 400 · partial — A lack of conformity in goods covered by Directive (EU) 2019/771 becomes apparent eight months after delivery. What does Article 11(1) presume, and when does that presumption not apply?

gold-sgd-one-year-presumption · answered

System answer

Any lack of conformity which becomes apparent within one year of the time when the goods were delivered shall be presumed to have existed at the time when the goods were delivered.[1]
That presumption does not apply where it is proved otherwise or where the presumption is incompatible with the nature of the goods or with the nature of the lack of conformity.[1]

Reference answer

It presumes that the lack of conformity existed at delivery, including for goods with digital elements, unless the contrary is proved or the presumption is incompatible with the nature of the goods or the nature of the lack of conformity.

Q9 · Article structure 400 · partial — Goods sold under Directive (EU) 2019/771 are not in conformity. May the consumer choose between repair and replacement, and must that remedy be free of charge?

gold-sgd-repair-or-replacement · answered

System answer

Repairs or replacements shall be carried out free of charge.[2]

Reference answer

The consumer may choose repair or replacement in order to have the goods brought into conformity, unless the chosen remedy is impossible or would impose disproportionate costs on the seller in the circumstances listed in Article 13(2). Where repair or replacement is used, it must be carried out free of charge, within a reasonable time after the seller is informed, and without significant inconvenience to the consumer.

Q10 · Article structure 400 · correct — Under Directive (EU) 2019/771, may a consumer terminate the sales contract when the lack of conformity is only minor?

gold-sgd-minor-lack-no-termination · answered

System answer

Under Directive (EU) 2019/771, the consumer shall not be entitled to terminate the contract if the lack of conformity is only minor.[4]
The burden of proof with regard to whether the lack of conformity is minor shall be on the seller.[4]

Reference answer

No. The consumer is not entitled to terminate if the lack of conformity is only minor, and the seller bears the burden of proving that it is minor.

Q11 · Article structure 400 · wrong — How does Directive (EU) 2019/771 measure a price reduction for goods that are not in conformity?

gold-sgd-price-reduction · insufficient

System answer

当前知识库没有足够证据回答这个问题。

Reference answer

The reduction must be proportionate to the decrease in the value of the goods received by the consumer compared with the value the goods would have had if they had been in conformity.

Q14 · Article structure 400 · partial — Digital content covered by Directive (EU) 2019/770 and supplied in exchange for a price is not in conformity. What remedies does Article 14 provide, and when is termination unavailable because the defect is minor?

gold-dcd-conformity-remedies · answered

System answer

Under Article 14, in the case of a lack of conformity, the consumer is entitled to have the digital content or digital service brought into conformity, to receive a proportionate reduction in the price, or to terminate the contract, under the conditions set out in that Article.[2]
Where the digital content or digital service is supplied in exchange for a price, the consumer should be able to terminate the contract only if the lack of conformity is not minor.[1]

Reference answer

The consumer is entitled to have the digital content or service brought into conformity, to a proportionate price reduction, or to terminate the contract, under the conditions in Article 14. Where a price is paid, the consumer may terminate only if the lack of conformity is not minor, and the trader bears the burden of proving that it is minor.

Q15 · Article structure 400 · correct — A consumer covered by Directive (EU) 2019/770 tells the trader that the consumer is terminating the contract because the digital service is not in conformity. How quickly must the trader make a reimbursement owed under Article 14 or Article 16?

gold-dcd-reimbursement-time · answered

System answer

Any reimbursement owed to the consumer by the trader pursuant to Article 14(4) and (5) or 16(1) due to a price reduction or termination of the contract must be carried out without undue delay and, in any event, within 14 days of the date on which the trader is informed of the consumer's decision to invoke the right for a price reduction or to terminate the contract.[1]

Reference answer

The trader must reimburse the consumer without undue delay and, in any event, within 14 days of being informed of the consumer's decision to invoke the price reduction or to terminate.

Q16 · Article structure 400 · wrong — A contract under Directive (EU) 2019/770 supplies a digital service over a period of time. The trader wants to modify it beyond what is necessary to keep it in conformity with Articles 7 and 8. Which conditions in Article 19(1) must be met?

gold-dcd-modification-conditions · insufficient

System answer

当前知识库没有足够证据回答这个问题。

Reference answer

The contract must allow the modification and provide a valid reason for it. The modification must be made without additional cost to the consumer. The consumer must be informed of the modification in a clear and comprehensible manner. Where paragraph 2 applies, the consumer must also be informed reasonably in advance, on a durable medium, of the features and timing of the modification and of the right to terminate or of the possibility to keep the service without the modification.

Q17 · Article structure 400 · wrong — Under the original text of Directive 2005/29/EC, when is a commercial practice unfair under the general test in Article 5(2)?

gold-ucpd-two-part-unfairness-test · insufficient

System answer

当前知识库没有足够证据回答这个问题。

Reference answer

It is unfair when both connected parts are met: it is contrary to the requirements of professional diligence, and it materially distorts, or is likely to materially distort, the economic behaviour of the average consumer it reaches or to whom it is addressed, or of the average member of the group when it is directed to a particular group.

Q18 · Article structure 400 · correct — What must be shown before Directive 2005/29/EC treats a commercial practice as aggressive?

gold-ucpd-aggressive-practice · answered

System answer

A commercial practice is regarded as aggressive if, in its factual context, taking account of all its features and circumstances, by harassment, coercion, including the use of physical force, or undue influence, it significantly impairs or is likely to significantly impair the average consumer's freedom of choice or conduct with regard to the product and thereby causes him or is likely to cause him to take a transactional decision that he would not have taken otherwise.[1]

Reference answer

In its factual context, and taking account of all its features and circumstances, the practice must use harassment, coercion, including physical force, or undue influence. It must significantly impair, or be likely to significantly impair, the average consumer's freedom of choice or conduct with regard to the product, and thereby cause or be likely to cause a transactional decision that the consumer would not otherwise have taken.

Q19 · Article structure 400 · wrong — A trader makes an invitation to purchase covered by Directive 2005/29/EC, and the price information is not already apparent. What price information does Article 7 treat as material?

gold-ucpd-invitation-price-information · insufficient

System answer

当前知识库没有足够证据回答这个问题。

Reference answer

The price inclusive of taxes is material. If the price cannot reasonably be calculated in advance, the manner of calculating it is material, together with additional freight, delivery or postal charges where appropriate, or the fact that such charges may be payable where those charges also cannot reasonably be calculated in advance.

Q20 · Article structure 400 · partial — Under Directive 2005/29/EC, is a pyramid promotional scheme described in Annex I unfair only after a case-by-case assessment, or in all circumstances?

gold-ucpd-pyramid-scheme · answered

System answer

Annex I contains the list of commercial practices which shall in all circumstances be regarded as unfair.[2]
These are the only commercial practices which can be deemed unfair without a case-by-case assessment against Articles 5 to 9.[1]
Establishing, operating or promoting a pyramid promotional scheme is included in Annex I.[3]
Annex I is titled commercial practices which are in all circumstances considered unfair.[5]

Reference answer

Annex I, point 14, covers establishing, operating or promoting a pyramid scheme in which a consumer gives consideration for the opportunity to receive compensation derived primarily from introducing other consumers rather than from the sale or consumption of products. Article 5(5) says the Annex I practices are unfair in all circumstances. The same list applies in every Member State and may be changed only by revision of the Directive.

ANNEX I
COMMERCIAL PRACTICES WHICH ARE IN ALL CIRCUMSTANCES CONSIDERED UNFAIR
Misleading commercial practices
Q21 · Article structure 400 · partial — Under Council Directive 93/13/EEC, when is a contract term regarded as unfair, and when is a term treated as not individually negotiated?

gold-utd-unfair-term-test · answered

System answer

A contractual term which has not been individually negotiated shall be regarded as unfair if, contrary to the requirement of good faith, it causes a significant imbalance in the parties' rights and obligations arising under the contract, to the detriment of the consumer.[1]

Reference answer

A term that has not been individually negotiated is unfair if, contrary to good faith, it causes a significant imbalance in the parties' rights and obligations under the contract, to the detriment of the consumer. A term is always regarded as not individually negotiated where it was drafted in advance and the consumer has not been able to influence its substance, particularly in a pre-formulated standard contract.

用户明确授权填写；本条为Codex AI辅助评分，未经逐题人工确认。按冻结参考答案及必要事实的严格完整性口径评定。只答不公平测试，未答预先拟定且消费者不能影响内容的未单独协商标准。

Claim 1: supported

A contractual term which has not been individually negotiated shall be regarded as unfair if, contrary to the requirement of good faith, it causes a significant imbalance in the parties' rights and obligations arising under the contract, to the detriment of the consumer.

主张可在其绑定引用中直接定位。第3条要求非单独协商、违反诚信、显著失衡且损害消费者；预拟且不可影响是独立定义。
Saved source evidence

[1] Council Directive 93/13/EEC of 5 April 1993 on unfair terms in consumer contracts · [9844, 10132)

Article 3
1. A contractual term which has not been individually negotiated shall be regarded as unfair if, contrary to the requirement of good faith, it causes a significant imbalance in the parties' rights and obligations arising under the contract, to the detriment of the consumer.

Q22 · Article structure 400 · correct — A seller offers a consumer a written standard contract covered by Directive 93/13/EEC. What does Article 5 require for the wording, and how is doubt about a term resolved?

gold-utd-plain-language · answered

System answer

Article 5 requires that, where all or certain terms offered to the consumer are in writing, those terms must always be drafted in plain, intelligible language.[1]
Where there is doubt about the meaning of a term, the interpretation most favourable to the consumer shall prevail.[1]
This rule on interpretation does not apply in the context of the procedures laid down in Article 7(2).[1]

Reference answer

The written terms must always be drafted in plain, intelligible language. If there is doubt about the meaning of a term, the interpretation most favourable to the consumer prevails. That interpretation rule does not apply in the procedures laid down in Article 7(2).

Q23 · Article structure 400 · correct — Under Directive 93/13/EEC, what effect must national law give to an unfair term in a consumer contract, and what happens to the rest of the contract?

gold-utd-unfair-term-not-binding · answered

System answer

Member States shall lay down that unfair terms used in a contract concluded with a consumer by a seller or supplier shall, as provided for under their national law, not be binding on the consumer.[3]
The contract shall continue to bind the parties upon those terms if it is capable of continuing in existence without the unfair terms.[3]

Reference answer

Member States must provide that an unfair term used in a contract with a consumer is, as provided for under their national law, not binding on the consumer. The contract continues to bind the parties on the remaining terms if it is capable of continuing in existence without the unfair terms.

Q24 · Article structure 400 · correct — A seller claims that a standard term in a consumer contract covered by Directive 93/13/EEC was individually negotiated. Who must prove that claim?

gold-utd-negotiation-burden · answered

System answer

Where a seller or supplier claims that a standard term has been individually negotiated, the burden of proof in this respect is incumbent on the seller or supplier.[1]

Reference answer

The seller or supplier who makes that claim bears the burden of proof.

Q25 · Article structure 400 · correct refusal — What fixed amount in euro must an air carrier pay a passenger when it cancels a flight?

gold-refuse-air-cancellation-amount · insufficient

System answer

当前知识库没有足够证据回答这个问题。

Reference answer

None of the five frozen directives states a fixed euro amount payable by an air carrier for cancellation of a flight. Directive 2011/83/EU excludes passenger transport services from its scope except Article 8(2) and Articles 19 and 22, and those provisions concern information, payment fees and additional payments rather than a cancellation tariff. A recital mentions a compensation regime for service aspects of mixed contracts but states no airline amount. The goods, digital-content, unfair-term and unfair-practice directives do not set one either.

Q26 · Article structure 400 · correct refusal — What standard percentage of value added tax must a trader add to the price of consumer goods?

gold-refuse-standard-vat-rate · insufficient

System answer

当前知识库没有足够证据回答这个问题。

Reference answer

The frozen corpus contains no value-added-tax rate and no rule stating the percentage a trader must add. Price provisions, such as the requirement that an invitation to purchase show the price inclusive of taxes, do not state a tax percentage.

Q27 · Article structure 400 · correct refusal — What annual statutory interest rate must a trader add when reimbursing a consumer under Article 13 of Directive 2011/83/EU?

gold-refuse-refund-interest-rate · insufficient

System answer

当前知识库没有足够证据回答这个问题。

Reference answer

Article 13 of Directive 2011/83/EU requires reimbursement of the payments it identifies and, in a later paragraph, use of the same means of payment without fees, but it states no interest rate. The other directives state reimbursement periods or prohibit fees on reimbursement, and the Annex to Directive 93/13/EEC discusses contract terms about interest charged for financial services. None of those provisions sets a statutory annual rate on an Article 13 reimbursement.

Q28 · Article structure 400 · correct refusal — What maximum fine in euro must Germany impose on a trader for using one unfair term in a consumer contract?

gold-refuse-german-fine · insufficient

System answer

当前知识库没有足够证据回答这个问题。

Reference answer

The penalty provisions in the frozen directives require Member States to provide effective, proportionate and dissuasive penalties or enforcement measures. They do not name Germany, and they do not set a maximum fine in euro for using an unfair term.

Q29 · Article structure 400 · correct refusal — How many days after a seller refuses repair does Directive (EU) 2019/771 require a consumer to file a lawsuit before the claim is lost?

gold-refuse-lawsuit-deadline · insufficient

System answer

当前知识库没有足够证据回答这个问题。

Reference answer

Directive (EU) 2019/771 makes the seller liable for a lack of conformity that becomes apparent within the periods in Article 10 and allows Member States to attach limitation periods to the remedies, provided those periods still let the consumer exercise them. It does not require a lawsuit, name a court, or state a number of days after refusal of repair by which proceedings must be filed.

THE EUROPEAN PARLIAMENT AND THE COUNCIL OF THE EUROPEAN UNION,
Q30 · Article structure 400 · correct refusal — What minimum insurance cover must a package-travel organiser hold for a consumer's trip?

gold-refuse-package-insurance · insufficient

System answer

当前知识库没有足够证据回答这个问题。

Reference answer

Directive 2011/83/EU places contracts within the package-travel directive outside its scope, and Directive 2005/29/EC lists package-travel legislation among sectoral information rules in Annex II. Neither provision, nor the goods, digital-content or unfair-term directives, states a minimum insurance amount or cover for a package-travel organiser.

Q1 · Fixed window 800 · correct — The parties to a sales contract have not agreed when the goods will be delivered. Under the original text of Directive 2011/83/EU, what is the trader's delivery obligation?

gold-crd-delivery-time · answered

System answer

Unless the parties have agreed otherwise on the time of delivery, the trader shall deliver the goods by transferring the physical possession or control of the goods to the consumer without undue delay, but not later than 30 days from the conclusion of the contract.[5]
If the parties have not agreed on a specific delivery date, the trader should deliver the goods as soon as possible, but in any event not later than 30 days from the day of the conclusion of the contract.[1]

Reference answer

The trader must deliver the goods by transferring physical possession or control to the consumer without undue delay, and not later than 30 days from the conclusion of the contract.

Q2 · Fixed window 800 · partial — A consumer withdraws from a distance contract under Directive 2011/83/EU and tells the trader in accordance with Article 11. What payments must the trader reimburse, and by when?

gold-crd-withdrawal-refund · answered

System answer

The trader shall reimburse all payments received from the consumer, including, if applicable, the costs of delivery.[1]
The reimbursement must be made without undue delay and in any event not later than 14 days from the day on which the trader is informed of the consumer's decision to withdraw from the contract in accordance with Article 11.[1]
The trader shall carry out the reimbursement using the same means of payment as the consumer used for the initial transaction, unless the consumer has expressly agreed otherwise and provided that the consumer does not incur any fees as a result of such reimbursement.[1]
The trader shall not be required to reimburse the supplementary costs if the consumer has expressly opted for a type of delivery other than the least expensive type of standard delivery offered by the trader.[4]

Reference answer

The trader must reimburse all payments received from the consumer, including delivery costs where applicable, without undue delay and not later than 14 days after being informed of the withdrawal. The trader need not reimburse supplementary delivery costs if the consumer expressly chose a delivery type other than the least expensive standard delivery offered by the trader. For a sales contract, unless the trader has offered to collect the goods, the trader may withhold that reimbursement until the goods are received back or the consumer supplies evidence of having sent them back, whichever is earliest.

Q3 · Fixed window 800 · correct — Under Directive 2011/83/EU, may a trader charge the consumer a fee for using a particular means of payment that is higher than the trader's own cost of accepting that means?

gold-crd-payment-fee-cap · answered

System answer

Member States shall prohibit traders from charging consumers, in respect of the use of a given means of payment, fees that exceed the cost borne by the trader for the use of such means.[5]
Traders should be prohibited from charging consumers fees that exceed the cost borne by the trader for the use of a certain means of payment.[1]

Reference answer

No. Member States must prohibit traders from charging consumers, for the use of a given means of payment, fees that exceed the cost borne by the trader for the use of that means.

Q4 · Fixed window 800 · correct — A trader concludes a distance contract covered by Directive 2011/83/EU but never gives the consumer the withdrawal information required by Article 6(1)(h). When does the withdrawal period expire?

gold-crd-withdrawal-information-omission · answered

System answer

If the trader has not provided the consumer with the information on the right of withdrawal as required by point (h) of Article 6(1), the withdrawal period expires 12 months from the end of the initial withdrawal period as determined in accordance with Article 9(2).[1]
If the trader provides the consumer with that information within 12 months from the day referred to in Article 9(2), the withdrawal period expires 14 days after the day upon which the consumer receives that information.[2]

Reference answer

It expires 12 months from the end of the initial withdrawal period determined under Article 9(2).

Q5 · Fixed window 800 · partial — Does Directive 2011/83/EU always give a consumer 14 days to withdraw from a distance contract for digital content that is not supplied on a tangible medium?

gold-crd-withdrawal-digital-content · answered

System answer

For contracts for digital content not supplied on a tangible medium, the consumer should have a right of withdrawal unless he has consented to the beginning of the performance of the contract during the withdrawal period and has acknowledged that he will consequently lose the right to withdraw from the contract.[3]

Reference answer

No. Article 9(1) gives 14 days to withdraw from a distance or off-premises contract without giving a reason, but only where an Article 16 exception does not apply. Article 16(m) excludes that right once performance of digital content not supplied on a tangible medium has begun with the consumer's prior express consent and acknowledgment that the right of withdrawal is lost.

Q6 · Fixed window 800 · partial — Under Directive 2011/83/EU, the parties did not agree a delivery date. The trader misses the Article 18(1) delivery period. The consumer then sets an additional period appropriate to the circumstances, and the trader still does not deliver. May the consumer terminate, and what must the trader reimburse?

gold-crd-late-delivery-termination · answered

System answer

Where the parties have not agreed a delivery date, the trader should deliver the goods as soon as possible, but in any event not later than 30 days from the day of the conclusion of the contract.[4]
Where the trader has failed to deliver within the period set out in paragraph 1, the consumer shall call upon the trader to make the delivery within an additional period of time appropriate to the circumstances.[1]
If the trader fails to deliver the goods within that additional period of time, the consumer shall be entitled to terminate the contract.[1]
The consumer should call upon the trader to make the delivery within a reasonable additional period of time and be entitled to terminate the contract if the trader fails to deliver the goods even within that additional period of time.[2]

Reference answer

Yes. If the trader does not deliver within that additional period, the consumer may terminate the contract. Upon termination, the trader must reimburse all sums paid under the contract without undue delay. This answer concerns that additional-period route.

Q7 · Fixed window 800 · partial — Under the original text of Directive (EU) 2019/771, for how long is a seller liable for a lack of conformity that already exists when the goods are delivered? May a Member State provide a longer period, and may the period be shorter for second-hand goods?

gold-sgd-two-year-liability · answered

System answer

The seller is liable to the consumer for any lack of conformity which exists at the time when the goods were delivered and which becomes apparent within two years of that time.[3]
Member States may maintain or introduce longer time limits than those referred to in paragraphs 1 and 2 of Article 10.[2]
Member States should be allowed to enable the parties to agree on a shortened liability or limitation period for second-hand goods.[1]

Reference answer

The seller is liable where that lack of conformity becomes apparent within two years of delivery. Without prejudice to Article 7(3), that rule also applies to goods with digital elements. Member States may maintain or introduce longer time limits than those in Article 10(1) and (2). For second-hand goods, Member States may allow the seller and the consumer to agree a shorter liability or limitation period than those in paragraphs 1, 2 and 5, but that shorter period must not be less than one year.

Q8 · Fixed window 800 · correct — A lack of conformity in goods covered by Directive (EU) 2019/771 becomes apparent eight months after delivery. What does Article 11(1) presume, and when does that presumption not apply?

gold-sgd-one-year-presumption · answered

System answer

Article 11(1) provides that any lack of conformity which becomes apparent within one year of the time when the goods were delivered shall be presumed to have existed at the time when the goods were delivered, unless proved otherwise or unless this presumption is incompatible with the nature of the goods or with the nature of the lack of conformity.[1]
This paragraph also applies to goods with digital elements.[1]
The presumption does not apply where it is proved otherwise or where it is incompatible with the nature of the goods or with the nature of the lack of conformity.[1]

Reference answer

It presumes that the lack of conformity existed at delivery, including for goods with digital elements, unless the contrary is proved or the presumption is incompatible with the nature of the goods or the nature of the lack of conformity.

Q9 · Fixed window 800 · partial — Goods sold under Directive (EU) 2019/771 are not in conformity. May the consumer choose between repair and replacement, and must that remedy be free of charge?

gold-sgd-repair-or-replacement · answered

System answer

Repairs or replacements shall be carried out free of charge.[1]
Repairs or replacements shall be carried out within a reasonable period of time from the moment the seller has been informed by the consumer about the lack of conformity.[1]
Repairs or replacements shall be carried out without any significant inconvenience to the consumer, taking into account the nature of the goods and the purpose for which the consumer required the goods.[1]

Reference answer

The consumer may choose repair or replacement in order to have the goods brought into conformity, unless the chosen remedy is impossible or would impose disproportionate costs on the seller in the circumstances listed in Article 13(2). Where repair or replacement is used, it must be carried out free of charge, within a reasonable time after the seller is informed, and without significant inconvenience to the consumer.

Q10 · Fixed window 800 · correct — Under Directive (EU) 2019/771, may a consumer terminate the sales contract when the lack of conformity is only minor?

gold-sgd-minor-lack-no-termination · answered

System answer

The consumer shall not be entitled to terminate the contract if the lack of conformity is only minor.[2]
The burden of proof with regard to whether the lack of conformity is minor shall be on the seller.[2]
The consumer should enjoy the right to terminate the contract only in cases where the lack of conformity is not minor.[4]

Reference answer

No. The consumer is not entitled to terminate if the lack of conformity is only minor, and the seller bears the burden of proving that it is minor.

Q11 · Fixed window 800 · wrong — How does Directive (EU) 2019/771 measure a price reduction for goods that are not in conformity?

gold-sgd-price-reduction · insufficient

System answer

当前知识库没有足够证据回答这个问题。

Reference answer

The reduction must be proportionate to the decrease in the value of the goods received by the consumer compared with the value the goods would have had if they had been in conformity.

Subject matter and purpose

Q14 · Fixed window 800 · wrong — Digital content covered by Directive (EU) 2019/770 and supplied in exchange for a price is not in conformity. What remedies does Article 14 provide, and when is termination unavailable because the defect is minor?

gold-dcd-conformity-remedies · answered

System answer

Under Article 14, in the case of a lack of conformity the consumer is entitled to have the digital content or digital service brought into conformity, to receive a proportionate reduction in the price, or to terminate the contract, under the conditions set out in that Article.[1]
The consumer is entitled to have the digital content or digital service brought into conformity unless this would be impossible or would impose costs on the trader.[1]
Where the digital content or digital service is supplied in exchange for a price, the consumer should be able to terminate the contract only if the lack of conformity is not minor.[2]

Reference answer

The consumer is entitled to have the digital content or service brought into conformity, to a proportionate price reduction, or to terminate the contract, under the conditions in Article 14. Where a price is paid, the consumer may terminate only if the lack of conformity is not minor, and the trader bears the burden of proving that it is minor.

Codex AI（用户授权辅助评分）

用户明确授权填写；本条为Codex AI辅助评分，未经逐题人工确认。按冻结参考答案及必要事实的严格完整性口径评定。把“成本不成比例”写成只要“产生成本”即可拒绝恢复合格，实质扩大商家免责范围；亦漏举证责任。

Claim 1: supported

Under Article 14, in the case of a lack of conformity the consumer is entitled to have the digital content or digital service brought into conformity, to receive a proportionate reduction in the price, or to terminate the contract, under the conditions set out in that Article.

已对照其绑定引用核查语义与条件。区分恢复合格、减价、解除，恢复合格的例外是不可行或成本不成比例，非任何成本。

Claim 2: not supported

The consumer is entitled to have the digital content or digital service brought into conformity unless this would be impossible or would impose costs on the trader.

引文在“costs on the tr”处截断，答案把免责条件写成产生成本，遗漏关键的“不成比例”。

Claim 3: supported

Where the digital content or digital service is supplied in exchange for a price, the consumer should be able to terminate the contract only if the lack of conformity is not minor.

主张可在其绑定引用中直接定位。区分恢复合格、减价、解除，恢复合格的例外是不可行或成本不成比例，非任何成本。

Q15 · Fixed window 800 · correct — A consumer covered by Directive (EU) 2019/770 tells the trader that the consumer is terminating the contract because the digital service is not in conformity. How quickly must the trader make a reimbursement owed under Article 14 or Article 16?

gold-dcd-reimbursement-time · answered

System answer

Any reimbursement owed to the consumer by the trader pursuant to Article 14(4) and (5) or 16(1), due to a price reduction or termination of the contract, shall be carried out without undue delay and, in any event, within 14 days of the date on which the trader is informed of the consumer's decision to invoke the consumer's right for a price reduction or to terminate the contract.[1][2]

Reference answer

The trader must reimburse the consumer without undue delay and, in any event, within 14 days of being informed of the consumer's decision to invoke the price reduction or to terminate.

Q16 · Fixed window 800 · correct — A contract under Directive (EU) 2019/770 supplies a digital service over a period of time. The trader wants to modify it beyond what is necessary to keep it in conformity with Articles 7 and 8. Which conditions in Article 19(1) must be met?

gold-dcd-modification-conditions · answered

System answer

The trader may modify the digital content or digital service beyond what is necessary to maintain conformity in accordance with Articles 7 and 8 if the conditions in Article 19(1) are met.[1]
The contract must allow, and provide a valid reason for, such a modification.[1]
The modification must be made without additional cost to the consumer.[1]
The consumer must be informed in a clear and comprehensible manner of the modification.[1]
In the cases referred to in paragraph 2, the consumer must be informed reasonably in advance on a durable medium of the features and time of the modification and of the right to terminate the contract in accordance with paragraph 2, or of the possibility to maintain the digital content or digital service without such a modification.[1]

Reference answer

The contract must allow the modification and provide a valid reason for it. The modification must be made without additional cost to the consumer. The consumer must be informed of the modification in a clear and comprehensible manner. Where paragraph 2 applies, the consumer must also be informed reasonably in advance, on a durable medium, of the features and timing of the modification and of the right to terminate or of the possibility to keep the service without the modification.

Q17 · Fixed window 800 · partial — Under the original text of Directive 2005/29/EC, when is a commercial practice unfair under the general test in Article 5(2)?

gold-ucpd-two-part-unfairness-test · answered

System answer

Under Article 5(2), a commercial practice is unfair if it is contrary to the requirements of professional diligence and it materially distorts or is likely to materially distort the economic behaviour of the average consumer.[3]

Reference answer

It is unfair when both connected parts are met: it is contrary to the requirements of professional diligence, and it materially distorts, or is likely to materially distort, the economic behaviour of the average consumer it reaches or to whom it is addressed, or of the average member of the group when it is directed to a particular group.

Q18 · Fixed window 800 · correct — What must be shown before Directive 2005/29/EC treats a commercial practice as aggressive?

gold-ucpd-aggressive-practice · answered

System answer

A commercial practice shall be regarded as aggressive if, in its factual context, taking account of all its features and circumstances, by harassment, coercion, including the use of physical force, or undue influence, it significantly impairs or is likely to significantly impair the average consumer's freedom of choice or conduct with regard to the product and thereby causes him or is likely to cause him to take a transactional decision that he would not have taken otherwise.[1]

Reference answer

In its factual context, and taking account of all its features and circumstances, the practice must use harassment, coercion, including physical force, or undue influence. It must significantly impair, or be likely to significantly impair, the average consumer's freedom of choice or conduct with regard to the product, and thereby cause or be likely to cause a transactional decision that the consumer would not otherwise have taken.

Q19 · Fixed window 800 · wrong — A trader makes an invitation to purchase covered by Directive 2005/29/EC, and the price information is not already apparent. What price information does Article 7 treat as material?

gold-ucpd-invitation-price-information · insufficient

System answer

当前知识库没有足够证据回答这个问题。

Reference answer

The price inclusive of taxes is material. If the price cannot reasonably be calculated in advance, the manner of calculating it is material, together with additional freight, delivery or postal charges where appropriate, or the fact that such charges may be payable where those charges also cannot reasonably be calculated in advance.

Q20 · Fixed window 800 · partial — Under Directive 2005/29/EC, is a pyramid promotional scheme described in Annex I unfair only after a case-by-case assessment, or in all circumstances?

gold-ucpd-pyramid-scheme · answered

System answer

Annex I contains the full list of commercial practices which are in all circumstances unfair.[1]
These are the only commercial practices which can be deemed to be unfair without a case-by-case assessment against the provisions of Articles 5 to 9.[1]
Annex I lists establishing, operating or promoting a pyramid promotional scheme where a consumer gives consideration for the opportunity to receive compensation derived primarily from the introduction of other consumers into the scheme rather than from the sale or consumption of products.[3]

Reference answer

Annex I, point 14, covers establishing, operating or promoting a pyramid scheme in which a consumer gives consideration for the opportunity to receive compensation derived primarily from introducing other consumers rather than from the sale or consumption of products. Article 5(5) says the Annex I practices are unfair in all circumstances. The same list applies in every Member State and may be changed only by revision of the Directive.

Q21 · Fixed window 800 · correct — Under Council Directive 93/13/EEC, when is a contract term regarded as unfair, and when is a term treated as not individually negotiated?

gold-utd-unfair-term-test · answered

System answer

A contractual term which has not been individually negotiated shall be regarded as unfair if, contrary to the requirement of good faith, it causes a significant imbalance in the parties' rights and obligations arising under the contract, to the detriment of the consumer.[1]
A term shall always be regarded as not individually negotiated where it has been drafted in advance and the consumer has therefore not been able to influence the substance of the term, particularly in the context of a pre-formulated standard contract.[1]
Where any seller or supplier claims that a standard term has been individually negotiated, the burden of proof in this respect shall be incumbent on him.[3]

Reference answer

A term that has not been individually negotiated is unfair if, contrary to good faith, it causes a significant imbalance in the parties' rights and obligations under the contract, to the detriment of the consumer. A term is always regarded as not individually negotiated where it was drafted in advance and the consumer has not been able to influence its substance, particularly in a pre-formulated standard contract.

2. A term shall always be regarded as not individually negotiated where it has been drafted in advance and the consumer has therefore not been able to influence the substance of the term, particularly in the context of a pre-formulated standard contract.

Q22 · Fixed window 800 · wrong — A seller offers a consumer a written standard contract covered by Directive 93/13/EEC. What does Article 5 require for the wording, and how is doubt about a term resolved?

gold-utd-plain-language · insufficient

System answer

当前知识库没有足够证据回答这个问题。

Reference answer

The written terms must always be drafted in plain, intelligible language. If there is doubt about the meaning of a term, the interpretation most favourable to the consumer prevails. That interpretation rule does not apply in the procedures laid down in Article 7(2).

Q23 · Fixed window 800 · correct — Under Directive 93/13/EEC, what effect must national law give to an unfair term in a consumer contract, and what happens to the rest of the contract?

gold-utd-unfair-term-not-binding · answered

System answer

Under Article 6(1), Member States shall lay down that unfair terms used in a contract concluded with a consumer by a seller or supplier shall, as provided for under their national law, not be binding on the consumer.[2]
The contract shall continue to bind the parties upon those terms if it is capable of continuing in existence without the unfair terms.[2][1]

Reference answer

Member States must provide that an unfair term used in a contract with a consumer is, as provided for under their national law, not binding on the consumer. The contract continues to bind the parties on the remaining terms if it is capable of continuing in existence without the unfair terms.

Q25 · Fixed window 800 · correct refusal — What fixed amount in euro must an air carrier pay a passenger when it cancels a flight?

gold-refuse-air-cancellation-amount · insufficient

System answer

当前知识库没有足够证据回答这个问题。

Reference answer

None of the five frozen directives states a fixed euro amount payable by an air carrier for cancellation of a flight. Directive 2011/83/EU excludes passenger transport services from its scope except Article 8(2) and Articles 19 and 22, and those provisions concern information, payment fees and additional payments rather than a cancellation tariff. A recital mentions a compensation regime for service aspects of mixed contracts but states no airline amount. The goods, digital-content, unfair-term and unfair-practice directives do not set one either.

Q26 · Fixed window 800 · correct refusal — What standard percentage of value added tax must a trader add to the price of consumer goods?

gold-refuse-standard-vat-rate · insufficient

System answer

当前知识库没有足够证据回答这个问题。

Reference answer

The frozen corpus contains no value-added-tax rate and no rule stating the percentage a trader must add. Price provisions, such as the requirement that an invitation to purchase show the price inclusive of taxes, do not state a tax percentage.

Q27 · Fixed window 800 · correct refusal — What annual statutory interest rate must a trader add when reimbursing a consumer under Article 13 of Directive 2011/83/EU?

gold-refuse-refund-interest-rate · insufficient

System answer

当前知识库没有足够证据回答这个问题。

Reference answer

Article 13 of Directive 2011/83/EU requires reimbursement of the payments it identifies and, in a later paragraph, use of the same means of payment without fees, but it states no interest rate. The other directives state reimbursement periods or prohibit fees on reimbursement, and the Annex to Directive 93/13/EEC discusses contract terms about interest charged for financial services. None of those provisions sets a statutory annual rate on an Article 13 reimbursement.

Q28 · Fixed window 800 · correct refusal — What maximum fine in euro must Germany impose on a trader for using one unfair term in a consumer contract?

gold-refuse-german-fine · insufficient

System answer

当前知识库没有足够证据回答这个问题。

Reference answer

The penalty provisions in the frozen directives require Member States to provide effective, proportionate and dissuasive penalties or enforcement measures. They do not name Germany, and they do not set a maximum fine in euro for using an unfair term.

Q29 · Fixed window 800 · correct refusal — How many days after a seller refuses repair does Directive (EU) 2019/771 require a consumer to file a lawsuit before the claim is lost?

gold-refuse-lawsuit-deadline · insufficient

System answer

当前知识库没有足够证据回答这个问题。

Reference answer

Directive (EU) 2019/771 makes the seller liable for a lack of conformity that becomes apparent within the periods in Article 10 and allows Member States to attach limitation periods to the remedies, provided those periods still let the consumer exercise them. It does not require a lawsuit, name a court, or state a number of days after refusal of repair by which proceedings must be filed.

Q30 · Fixed window 800 · correct refusal — What minimum insurance cover must a package-travel organiser hold for a consumer's trip?

gold-refuse-package-insurance · insufficient

System answer

当前知识库没有足够证据回答这个问题。

Reference answer

Directive 2011/83/EU places contracts within the package-travel directive outside its scope, and Directive 2005/29/EC lists package-travel legislation among sectoral information rules in Annex II. Neither provision, nor the goods, digital-content or unfair-term directives, states a minimum insurance amount or cover for a package-travel organiser.

CONSUMER INFORMATION FOR CONTRACTS OTHER THAN DISTANCE OR OFF-PREMISES CONTRACTS

Article 5
Information requirements for contracts other than distance or off-premises contracts
1. Before the consumer is bound by a contract other than a distance or an off-premises contract, or any corresponding offer, the trader shall provide the consumer with the following information in a clear and comprehensible manner, if that information is not already apparent from the context:
(a) the main characteristics of the goods or services, to the extent appropriate to the medium and to the goods or services;
(b) the identity of the trader, such as his trading name, the geographical address at which he is established and his telephone number;
(c) the total price of the goods or services inclusive of taxes,
Q1 · Article structure 800 · correct — The parties to a sales contract have not agreed when the goods will be delivered. Under the original text of Directive 2011/83/EU, what is the trader's delivery obligation?

gold-crd-delivery-time · answered

System answer

Under Directive 2011/83/EU, unless the parties have agreed otherwise on the time of delivery, the trader shall deliver the goods by transferring the physical possession or control of the goods to the consumer without undue delay, but not later than 30 days from the conclusion of the contract.[3]
If the parties have not agreed on a specific delivery date, the trader should deliver the goods as soon as possible, but in any event not later than 30 days from the day of the conclusion of the contract.[1]

Reference answer

The trader must deliver the goods by transferring physical possession or control to the consumer without undue delay, and not later than 30 days from the conclusion of the contract.
